早期共和国在公共建筑上最为成功，其影响也在这里持续得最久，因为直到 1840 年，仍有一批设计良好的古典模式建筑在建造，如纽约的辅助国库大楼（Subtreasury building）。纽约的麦库姆（McComb）、康涅狄格的霍德利（Hoadley）、宾夕法尼亚和马里兰的拉特罗布（Latrobe）——仅举几位领衔的建筑师——他们的作品代表着美国专业设计的高水位线；而华盛顿的国会大厦尽管经手众多，却仍是一座相当连贯的构筑物，这一事实见证了他们传统的力量。然而，纵有种种细部的小小妙诣，我们切不可犯现代复兴派如菲斯克·金博尔先生那样的错误——他们敦促把美洲的古典传统接受为一种通用现代风格的基础。形式与功能在古典模式中相距太远，无法让一种在住宅与公共建筑、工厂与谷仓之间四脚着地、齐头并进的建筑生长出来；何况，现代世界有太多罗马或文艺复兴的建造者连隐约都不曾预见过的新结构。在中世纪建造中，市政厅与大教堂是两种不同的建筑：也许使用同样的元素，却营造出了全然不同的效果。而在早期共和国的建筑里，国库大楼可能是教堂，教堂可能是宅邸——就任何可以观察的外部分野而言——事实上，与那个时代的教堂相伴随的唯一宗教感情，是一种冷冰冰的自然神论，或一种同样冰冷、全然失落了中间诸世纪之记忆与联想的新教信仰。这种建筑达成秩序与尊严，不是靠调和差异，而是靠取消差异。它的标准不内在于建筑，而是外加于其上。当结构的用途恰巧与风格相合，结果可能在各方面都令人赞叹。当它不相合时，结果便沉闷而平庸；而且说实话，早期共和国的建筑很大一部分是沉闷而平庸的。
### IV / 四

===Original===
One further effect of the classic mode has still to be noted: the introduction of formal city design, by the French engineer, Major L’Enfant, in the laying out of Washington. Stirred by the memory of the grand design of Paris under Louis XIV, with its radiating avenues that cut through the city in the way that riding lanes cut through the hunting forest, L’Enfant sought to superimpose a dignified pattern upon the rectangular plan provided by the commissioners of Washington. By putting the major public buildings in key positions, by providing for a proper physical relation between the various departments of the government, by planning spacious avenues of approach, culminating in squares, triangles, and round-points, Major L’Enfant gave great dignity to the new capital city, and even though in the years that followed his plan was often ignored and overridden, it still maintained a monumental framework for the administrative buildings of the American State.
Unfortunately, if Washington has the coherence of a formal plan, it also has its abstractness: contrived to set off and serve the buildings of the government, it exercised no control over domestic building, over business, over the manifold economic functions of the developing city. The framework was excellent, if cities could live by government alone. By laying too much stress on formal order, the exponents of classic taste paved the way for the all too formal order of the gridiron plan, and since the gridiron development was suited to hasty commercial exploitation, while the mode of Washington was not, it was in this mold that the architecture of the nineteenth century was cast.
Within a short while after its introduction in New York in 1811 the effects of the rectangular streets and rectangular lots became evident; whereas the prints of New York before 1825 show a constant variety in the elevation and layout of houses, those after this date resemble more and more standardized boxes. Long monotonous streets that terminated nowhere, filled by rows of monotonous houses﻿—this was the net contribution of the formal plan. Classical taste was not responsible for these enormities﻿—but on the whole it did nothing to check them, and since the thrifty merchants of New York could not understand L’Enfant’s plan for Washington, they seized upon that part of it which was intelligible: its regularity, its appearance of order.
With the new forces that were at work on the American scene, with the disintegration of classical culture under the combined influence of pioneer enterprise, mechanical invention, overseas commerce, and the almost religious cult of utilitarianism, all this was indeed inevitable. What happened to the proud, Roman-patterned republic of 1789 is a matter of common knowledge. Benjamin Latrobe, the British architect who contributed so much to the Capitol at Washington﻿—including a new order of corn stalks and tobacco leaves﻿—was a witness to the disintegration of the age and the dissolution of its world of ideas; and there is a familiar ring to his commentary upon it:
===Chinese===
古典模式还有一个后果须得记下：由法国工程师朗方（L'Enfant）少校在华盛顿的规划中引入的正式城市设计。受到路易十四治下巴黎宏大设计之记忆的激励——那些放射状大道切开城市，正像骑马道切开狩猎林——朗方试图在华盛顿专员们提供的矩形方格平面之上，叠加一个庄严的图式。通过把主要公共建筑置于关键位置，通过为政府各部门之间规划恰当的物质关系，通过设计宽阔的进谒大道、汇聚于广场、三角地与环形交叉口，朗方少校赋予了新首都巨大的尊严；即便在随后的岁月里他的规划常被忽视、被僭改，它仍为美利坚国家的行政建筑维持了一个纪念性的框架。
不幸的是，华盛顿若有正式规划的连贯，它也就有正式规划的抽象：它为衬托和服务政府建筑而设计，对住宅建筑、对商业、对发展中城市纷繁的经济功能，却未加任何控制。倘若城市能单靠政府过活，这个框架本是出色的。古典趣味的鼓吹者们过分强调形式秩序，为方格网规划那种过于形式的秩序铺平了道路；既然方格网的发展适合仓促的商业榨取，而华盛顿的模式不适合，十九世纪的建筑便铸进了这只模子。
1811 年方格街道与方格地块在纽约引入之后不久，其后果便显露出来：1825 年以前的纽约版画，显示住宅的立面与布局变化不断，而此后的版画越来越像标准化的盒子。哪里也到不了的长而单调的街道，填满一排排单调的住宅——这便是正式规划的净贡献。古典趣味对这些恶行不负责任——但大体上它也毫无制止；而且，既然节俭的纽约商人们理解不了朗方为华盛顿所作的规划，他们便抓住了其中可以理解的那一部分：它的规则性，它的秩序表象。
随着作用于美洲场景的新势力，随着古典文化在拓荒者企业、机械发明、海外商业与近乎宗教的功利主义崇拜的合力影响之下解体，这一切确乎无可避免。那个 1789 年骄傲的、以罗马为范型的共和国遭遇了什么，是人所共知的事。本杰明·拉特罗布（Benjamin Latrobe），这位对华盛顿国会大厦贡献良多的英国建筑师——包括一种玉米穗与烟叶的新柱式——亲见了那个时代的解体及其观念世界的消散；而他对这一切的评论，听来耳熟：

===Original===
> “I remember [he says in his autobiography] the time when I was over head and ears in love with Man in a State of Nature.﻿ ﻿… Social Compacts were my hobbies; the American Revolution﻿—I ask its pardon, for it deserves better company﻿—was a sort of dream of the Golden Age; and the French Revolution was the Golden Age itself. I should be ashamed to confess all this if I had not a thousand companions in my kaleidoscopic amusement, and those generally men of ardent, benevolent, and well-informed minds and excellent hearts. Alas! experience has destroyed the illusion, the kaleidoscope is broken, and all the tinsel of scenery that glittered so delightfully is translated and turned to raggedness. A dozen years’ residence at the Republican court of Washington had affected wonderfully the advance of riper years.”
===Chinese===
> 「我记得〔他在自传中说〕那个年代，我浑身心地恋爱着『自然状态中的人』……社会契约是我的癖好；美国革命——我请求它的原谅，因为它配得上更好的同伴——是黄金时代的一场梦；而法国革命就是黄金时代本身。若非在我的万花筒娱乐中有千百个同伴，而且多半是些热诚、仁厚、见识通达、心地优良之士，我本应羞于承认这一切。唉！经验毁掉了幻象，万花筒碎了，那曾经闪耀得如此悦目的布景金箔，已被译转成褴褛。在华盛顿共和宫廷的一十二年居留，对成熟年纪的长进，影响奇妙。」

===Original===
Major L’Enfant’s plan for Washington was the last gasp, it seems to me, of the classical order; Jefferson’s University of Virginia was perhaps its most perfect consummation, for Jefferson had planned for the life of the institution as well as for the shell which was to contain it. Before the nineteenth century was long under way men’s minds ceased to move freely within the classical idolum; and by 1860 the mood was obliterated and a large part of the work had been submerged or destroyed. The final ironic commentary upon the dignity and austerity of the earlier temples is illustrated in a house in Kennebunkport, Maine; for there the serene, pillared façade is broken up in the rear by a later, and alas! a necessary addition: a two-story bow-window projected far enough beyond the eaves to give a little light to the occupants of the rooms!
In sum, there was a pathetic incompatibility in this architecture between need and achievement, between pretensions and matter-of-fact﻿—a rigid opposition to common sense that a vernacular, however playful, would never countenance. These temples were built with the marmoreal gesture of eternity; they satisfied the desire and fashion of the moment; and today their ghosts parade before us, brave but incredible.
===Chinese===
在我看来，朗方少校的华盛顿规划，是古典秩序的最后一次喘息；杰斐逊的弗吉尼亚大学或许是其最完美的圆成，因为杰斐逊既为机构的生活作了规划，也为盛纳它的外壳作了规划。十九世纪还没走出多远，人的心灵就不再能在古典的幻象之内自由移动了；到 1860 年，这种心绪已被抹去，作品的很大一部分已没入水底或毁掉。对早期神庙之尊严与峻肃的最后一声反讽评注，显现在缅因州肯纳邦克波特（Kennebunkport）的一座宅子上：那里，宁静的列柱正立面，在背后被一段后来的——唉！——必不可少的加建打断：一座两层的凸窗，远远探出檐口之外，好给房间的住户透进一点光！
总之，这种建筑在需要与成就之间、在自负与实际之间，存在一种可悲的不能相容——一种对常识的僵硬对立，哪怕再活泼的乡土建筑也绝不会认可。这些神庙以大理石般永恒的姿态建造；它们满足的却是一时的欲望与时尚；今天，它们的鬼魂在我们面前列队走过，勇敢，却难以置信。

## V The Defeat of Romanticism / 浪漫主义的失败

### I / 一

===Original===
Between 1860 and 1890, some of the forces that were latent in industrialism were realized in American architecture. Where the first pioneers had fared timidly, hampered by insufficient resources, the generation that had been stimulated by war industries and profiteering, by the discovery of petroleum and natural gas, by the spanning of the American continent and by cable communication with Europe, rioted over its newfound wealth.

“The Song of the Broadax” still faintly lingered on the Pacific slopes; but the land pioneer was rapidly giving way to the pioneer in industry; and for perhaps the first time during the century, the surplus of capital outran the immediate demand for new plant and equipment. The Iron Age reached its peak of achievement in a series of great bridges, beginning with the Eads Bridge at St. Louis; and romanticism made a last stand. It will pay us, perhaps, to take one last look at the romantic effort, in order to see how impossible and hopeless was the task it set out to perform.

In England, the romantic movement in architecture had made the return to the Middle Ages a definite symbol of social reform: in Ruskin’s mind it was associated with the restoration of a medieval type of polity, something like a reformed manor, while with Morris it meant cutting loose from the machine and returning to the meticulous handicraft of the town-guilds. In America, the romantic movement lacked these social and economic implications; and while it is not unfair to say that the literary expression of English romanticism was on the whole much better than the architecture, in the proportion that *The Stones of Venice* was better than the Ashmolean Museum or the Albert Memorial, the reverse is true on this side of the Atlantic.

Inarticulate as H. H. Richardson, the chief exponent of American romanticism, was, it seemed for a while as if he might breast the tide of mechanical industry and create for a good part of the scene a sense of stability and harmony which it had all too plainly lacked. In relation to his age, however, Richardson was in the biological sense a “sport”; surrounded by jerry-builders, who had degraded the craft of building, and engineers who ignored it, he was perhaps the last of the great medieval line of master-masons.

Richardson began his career in America directly after the Civil War. Almost the first of the new generation of Americans to be trained by the École des Beaux Arts, he brought back to America none of those atrocious adaptations of the French Renaissance like the New York, Philadelphia, and Boston Post Offices. On the contrary, he had come under the influence of Viollet-le-Duc; and for about ten years he struggled with incongruous forms and materials in the anomalous manner known as Free Gothic. The end of this period of experiment came in 1872, when he received the commission for Trinity Church in Boston; and although it was not until ten years later that he saw any Romanesque buildings other than in photographs﻿—for he had not traveled during his student-years in Paris﻿—it was in this sturdy mode that he cast his best work. Richardson was not a decorator, but a builder: in going back to Romanesque precedent, with its round arches and massive stone members, he was following out a dictum of Viollet-le-Duc’s: “only primitive sources supply the energy for a long career.” Turning away from “applied Gothic,” Richardson started to build from the bottom up. So far had the art of masonry disappeared that in Trinity Church Richardson sometimes introduced struts and girders without any attempt to assimilate them in the composition; but as far as any single man could absorb and live with a vanished tradition, Richardson did.

===Chinese===
在 1860 到 1890 年之间，工业主义中一些潜在的力量在美国建筑里实现了。早期拓荒者受资源匮乏所限、走得畏畏缩缩；而这一代人在军工产业与战争暴利的刺激下，在石油与天然气的发现中，在横贯美国大陆的交通和与欧洲的电报通讯中，向着新到手的财富纵情狂欢。

《宽斧之歌》的余音还在太平洋坡地上微微回荡；但土地上的拓荒者正迅速让位给工业里的拓荒者；而且大约是本世纪头一回，资本的节余超出了对新厂房与新设备的即时需求。钢铁时代的成就登峰造极于一系列大桥，始于圣路易斯（St. Louis）的伊兹桥（Eads Bridge）；浪漫主义进行了最后的抵抗。为要看清它着手执行的任务是何等无望、何等不可能，我们不妨对这番浪漫的努力作最后一次的注视，这或许对我们有好处。

在英格兰，建筑中的浪漫主义运动曾把回到中世纪当作社会改革的明确象征：在罗斯金（Ruskin）心里，它与恢复一种中世纪类型的政体相连，某种类似改革过的庄园的东西；在莫里斯（Morris）那里，它意味着摆脱机器、回到城市行会一丝不苟的手工技艺。在美国，浪漫主义运动缺少这些社会与经济的含义；虽说英国浪漫主义的文学表达总体上远胜其建筑——以《威尼斯之石》（The Stones of Venice）胜过阿什莫林博物馆（Ashmolean Museum）或阿尔伯特纪念碑（Albert Memorial）的比例而言——并不过分，但在大西洋的这一边，情形恰恰相反。

美国浪漫主义的主要阐释者 H·H·理查森（H. H. Richardson）尽管不善言辞，有一阵子，他似乎真能挺住机械工业的潮流，为这幅景象的相当大一部分创造出一种它显然一直欠缺的稳定与和谐之感。然而相对于他的时代，理查森在生物学的意义上是一个「突变」；四周是败坏了营造行当的偷工减料的营造商，是无视这门行当的工程师，他也许是伟大的中世纪石匠大师世系中最后的一位。

理查森的职业生涯恰在南北战争之后开始于美国。他几乎是第一代受教于巴黎美术学院的美国人，却没有把法国文艺复兴的那些骇人的翻版——像纽约、费城和波士顿的邮局——带回美国。相反，他受了维奥莱-勒-杜克（Viollet-le-Duc）的影响；约有十年光景，他以一种名为「自由哥特式」的奇异方式，同不相称的形式与材料苦苦搏斗。这一实验期结束于 1872 年，他接下波士顿三一教堂（Trinity Church）的委托；虽然要再过十年，他才在照片之外见到真正的罗曼式建筑——因为他在巴黎求学的年月里不曾旅行——他最好的作品恰恰是以这种敦实的模式铸成的。理查森不是装饰家，而是营造者：回到带有圆拱和厚重石构件的罗曼式先例，他是在践行维奥莱-勒-杜克的一句格言：「唯有本源能供给漫长生涯所需的活力。」撇开「外加的哥特式」，理查森开始从头往上盖。石工技艺消失得如此彻底，以致在三一教堂里，理查森有时安上撑杆和梁而不设法使它们融入构图；但就一个人所能吸收并与之共同生活于一种消逝的传统而言，理查森做到了极致。

===Original===
The proof of Richardson’s genius as a builder lies in the difference between the accepted drawings for Trinity Church and the finished building. His ideas altered with the progress of the work, and in almost every case the building itself is a vast improvement over the paper design. Moreover, in his capacity as master-mason, Richardson trained an able corps of craftsmen; and so pervasive was his influence that one still finds on houses Richardson never saw, the touches of delicate, leafy stone-carving he had introduced. With carving and sculpture, the other arts entered, and by his fine designs and exacting standards of work, Richardson elevated the position of the minor crafts, at the same time that he turned over unreservedly to men like John La Farge and Augustus St. Gaudens the major elements of decoration.

Probably most people who know Richardson’s name vaguely associate him with ecclesiastical work; but Richardson’s brand of romanticism was a genuine attempt to embrace the age, and in his long list of public works there are but five churches. If the Pittsburgh Court House and Trinity Church stand out as the hugest of his architectural conceptions, it is the smaller buildings that test the skill and imagination of the master, and the public libraries at North Easton, Malden, and Quincy, Mass., and some of the little railway stations in Massachusetts stand on an equally high level. Richardson pitted his own single powers against the barbarism of the Gilded Age; but, unlike his contemporaries in England, he did not turn his back upon the excellences of industrialism. “The things I want most to design,” he said to his biographer, “are a grain-elevator and the interior of a great river-steamboat.”

In short, Richardson sought to dominate his age. So nearly did he succeed that in a symposium on the ten finest buildings in America, conducted by an architectural journal in the ’eighties, Richardson was given five. This was no easy victory, and, to tell the truth, it was only a partial one. The case of the State Capitol at Albany, which Richardson and Eidlitz took in hand in 1878, after five million dollars had been squandered on it in the course of ten years’ misconstruction, scarcely caricatures the conditions under which the arts struggled to exist. Begun in the style of the Roman Renaissance, the building under Richardson’s impetuous touch began to take on Romanesque proportions, only to be legislated back into Renaissance by the offended lawgivers!

William Morris Hunt, then at the height of his powers, was commissioned to paint two large mural compositions for the assembly chamber of this blessed building. So much time had been spent in mismanaging the structure that Hunt was given only two months to transfer his cartouche to the panels; but he worked heroically, and, as one of his biographers says, the work was a great triumph. Great, perhaps﻿—but temporary! “The building had fallen into the hands of a political ring, and the poor construction was revealed in the leaking of the massive roof and the settling of the whole structure. Before ten years had passed, great portions of Hunt’s paintings flaked off, and what remained was walled up behind the rebuilding necessary to avert utter ruin.” In a period like this, Richardson’s comparative success takes on heroic proportions.

===Chinese===
理查森作为营造者的天才之证明，就在三一教堂获准的图纸与竣工建筑之间的差异里。他的构想随工程的进展而变，而且几乎在每一处，建筑本身都比纸面设计大有改进。再者，以其石匠大师的身份，理查森训练出一批能干的工匠队伍；他的影响如此无孔不入，以至于在他从未见过的房屋上，人们至今仍能发现他引入的那种细腻而多叶的石雕笔触。随着雕刻与雕塑进入，其他艺术也随之而来；理查森以精美的设计和严格的工作标准，提升了小手艺的地位，同时又把装饰的主要成分毫无保留地托付给约翰·拉法奇（John La Farge）与奥古斯塔斯·圣-高登斯（Augustus St. Gaudens）这样的人。

大概大多数知道理查森名字的人，都依稀把他与教堂建筑联系在一起；但理查森牌的浪漫主义是一次拥抱时代的真诚尝试，在他长长的公共作品清单上，教堂不过五座。如果说匹兹堡法院大楼（Pittsburgh Court House）和三一教堂是他最宏大的建筑构想，那么检验这位大师的技艺与想象力的，倒是那些较小的建筑：马萨诸塞州东北顿（North Easton）、莫尔登（Malden）和昆西（Quincy）的公共图书馆，以及马萨诸塞的几座小火车站，都站在同样高的水准上。理查森以一己之力对抗镀金时代（Gilded Age）的野蛮；但与他在英格兰的同代人不同，他没有背过身去不理会工业主义的种种长处。「我最想设计的，」他对他的传记作者说，「是一座谷物提升仓，和一艘大河轮船的内部。」

简言之，理查森想驾驭他的时代。他成功得如此接近，以至于在一家建筑期刊于八十年代举办的「美国十座最佳建筑」研讨会上，理查森一人独得五席。这不是轻易得来的胜利，而且说实话，也只是部分的胜利。奥尔巴尼（Albany）州议会大厦一案便足以写照诸艺术挣扎求存的境况——理查森与艾德利茨（Eidlitz）于 1878 年接手时，那座大厦已在十年的错误施工里糟蹋掉了五百万美元。它以罗马文艺复兴的风格起造，在理查森急切的拨弄下开始显出罗曼式的比例，结果却触怒了立法者，被立法强行拉回文艺复兴！

当时正当盛年的威廉·莫里斯·亨特（William Morris Hunt）受托为这座「有福」建筑的议事厅绘制两幅大型壁画。管理这座结构的荒废已耗去太多时间，亨特只剩两个月把他的饰框图样移上壁板；但他干得英勇，而且，正如他的一位传记作者所说，这件作品是一次伟大的胜利。伟大，或许吧——然而短暂！「大厦落入了政治集团之手，粗劣的施工从大屋顶的渗漏和整个结构的沉降中暴露出来。不出十年，亨特的画作大块剥落，余下的部分被封在为避免彻底坍塌而必要的重建工程之后。」在这样一个时期里，理查森那相对的成功，便有了史诗般的规模。

### II / 二

===Original===
With the little eddies of eclecticism, with the rage for the Mansard roof, or the introduction of German Gothic, and, a little later, the taste for Queen Anne domesticity, there is scarcely any need to deal; they represented only the dispersion of taste and the collapse of judgment which marked the Gilded Age.

Up to the time of the Chicago World’s Fair, Richardson had imitators, and they were not always mean ones. L. H. Buffington, in Minneapolis, had to his credit a number of buildings which would not, perhaps, have dishonored the master himself; but, as so often happens, the tags in Richardson’s work were easier to imitate than his spirit and inventiveness; and the chief marks of the style he created are the all-too-solid courses of rough stone, the round arch, the squat columns, and the contrasts in color between the light granite and the dark sandstone or serpentine. Mr. Montgomery Schuyler, an excellent architectural critic, once said, not without reason, that Richardson’s houses were not defensible except in a military sense; but one is tempted to read into these ponderous forms partly the architect’s unconscious desire to combat the infirmity and jerry-building of his lower contemporaries, and partly his patron’s anxiety to have a seat of refuge against the uneasy proletariat. A new feudalism was entrenching itself behind the stockades of Homestead and the other steel-towns of the Pittsburgh district. Here was a mode of building, solid, formidable, at times almost brutal, that served the esthetic needs of the barons of coal and steel almost as well as the classic met those heroes who had survived the War of Independence.

I have emphasized what was strong and fine in Richardson’s work in order to show how free it was from the minor faults of romanticism; and yet it reckoned without its host, and Richardson, alas! left scarcely a trace upon the period that followed. Romanticism was welcomed when it built churches; tolerated when it built libraries; petted when it built fine houses; but it could not go much farther. Richardson was a mason, and masonry was being driven out by steel; he was an original artist, and original art was being thrust into the background by connoisseurship and collection; he was a builder, and architecture was committing itself more and more to the paper plan; he insisted upon building foursquare, and building was doomed more and more to *façaderie*. The very strength of Richardson’s buildings was a fatal weakness in the growing centers of commerce and industry. It takes more than a little audacity to tear down one of Richardson’s monuments, and so, rather ironically, they have held their own against the insurrections of traffic and realty speculation; but the difficulty of getting rid of these Romanesque structures only increased the demand for a more frail and facile method of construction.

===Chinese===
至于折中主义的小小漩涡，对曼萨尔屋顶的狂热，德国哥特式的引入，以及稍后对安妮女王式家居趣味的嗜好，就无须多费笔墨了；它们代表的只是趣味的涣散与判断力的崩溃，那正是镀金时代的标记。

直到芝加哥世博会之前，理查森一直有模仿者，而且不总是拙劣的模仿者。明尼阿波利斯（Minneapolis）的 L·H·巴芬顿（L. H. Buffington）名下有几座建筑，或许不至于辱没大师本人；但正如常见的那样，理查森作品表面的标签总比他的精神与独创性容易模仿；他创造的这种风格的主要标志，是过于坚实的粗石层、圆拱、矮墩的柱子，以及浅色花岗岩与深色砂岩或蛇纹石之间的色彩对比。蒙哥马利·舒伊勒（Montgomery Schuyler）先生是位出色的建筑评论家，他曾说——不无道理——理查森的住宅除了军事意义之外无以自辩；但人们忍不住要在这些沉重的形式里，部分读出建筑师潜意识里对抗其低劣同代人羸弱施工与草率营造的愿望，部分读出其主顾为一座抵御不安分的无产者而设的庇护所的焦虑。一种新的封建主义，正在霍姆斯特德（Homestead）和匹兹堡地区其他钢铁重镇的壁垒后面掘壕自固。这便是一种营造方式：坚实、可畏、有时近乎粗暴，它服侍煤炭与钢铁男爵们的审美需要，几乎一如古典式曾经服侍那些从独立战争中生还的英雄。

我强调了理查森作品中强劲而精妙的东西，为的是显示它何其摆脱了浪漫主义的那些小毛病；然而它未曾算计到它的对手，而理查森，唉！几乎没有在其后的时代留下痕迹。浪漫主义建教堂时受欢迎；建图书馆时被容忍；建精美住宅时被宠爱；但它走不了更远。理查森是石匠，而石工正被钢材驱逐；他是独创的艺术家，而独创的艺术正被鉴赏与收藏挤到幕后；他是营造者，而建筑正日益托付给纸面规划；他坚持四四方方地建造，而营造正日益注定沦为「立面把戏」（façaderie）。理查森建筑之强，恰恰成了它在成长中的商业与工业中心里的致命弱点。拆掉理查森的一座纪念碑，需要不小的大胆，于是，颇具讽刺意味的是，它们顶住了交通与地产投机的叛乱而屹立不倒；但摆脱这些罗曼式建筑的困难，只增加了对一种更脆弱、更轻便的建造方法的需求。

===Original===
Romanticism met its great defeat in the office-building. By the use of the passenger elevator, first designed for an exhibition-tower adjacent to the Crystal Palace in 1853, it had become possible to raise the height of buildings to seven stories: the desire for ground-rents presently increased the height to ten. Beyond this, mere masonry could not go without thickening the supporting piers to such an extent that on a twenty-foot lot more than a quarter of the width would be lost on the lower floors. Richardson’s Marshall Field Building in Chicago was seven stories high; and that was about as far as solid stone or brick could climb without becoming undignified and futile by its bulk. The possibilities of masonry and the possibilities of commercial gain through ground-rents were at loggerheads, and by 1888 masonry was defeated.

Richardson, fortunately, did not live to see the undermining of the tradition he had founded and almost established. Within a decade of his death, however, only the empty forms of architecture remained, for the steel-cage of the engineer had become the new structural reality. By 1890 the ground-landlord had discovered, in the language of the pioneer’s favorite game, that “the roof’s the limit.” If that was so, why limit the roof? With this canny perception the skyscraper sprang into being.

During this Gilded Age the standard of the best building had risen almost as high as it had been in America in any earlier period; but the mass of good building had relatively decreased; and the domestic dwellings in both city and country lost those final touches of craftsmanship that had lingered, here and there, up to the Civil War. In the awkward country villas that began to fill the still-remote suburbs of the larger cities, all sense of style and proportion were lost: the plan was marked by meaningless irregularities; a dingy, muddy color spread over the wooden façades. There exists a huge and beautifully printed volume, of which, I believe, there are not more than a hundred copies, on the villas of Newport in 1876: the compiler thereof sought to satisfy the vanity of the original owners and the curiosity of a later generation; yet mid all these examples of the “novel” and the “unique,” there is not a single mansion that would satisfy any conceivable line of descendants.

===Chinese===
浪漫主义的大败仗，败在办公楼手里。乘客电梯——最初是为 1853 年水晶宫旁的一座展览塔设计的——的使用，使建筑的高度可以加到七层：对地租的渴望不久把高度加到了十层。再往上，纯然的砖石便无能为力，除非把承重的墩壁加厚到在二十英尺宽的宅基上，低层要损失四分之一以上的宽度。理查森在芝加哥的马歇尔·菲尔德大楼（Marshall Field Building）高七层；这大约就是实心的石头或砖不经由臃肿而失当、还能体面攀到的高度。石工的可能性与地租商业收益的可能性势不两立，到 1888 年，石工败下阵来。

所幸理查森没有活着看到他一手开创、几乎已然确立的传统被掏空。然而他死后不出十年，剩下的只有建筑空洞的形式，因为工程师的钢笼成了新的结构现实。到 1890 年，土地业主用拓荒者心爱的游戏里的话说，发现了「屋顶就是极限」。既然如此，为什么还要限制屋顶？凭着这精明的见识，摩天楼应运而生。

在这个镀金时代，最佳营造的水准几乎升到了美国以往任何时期的高度；但良好营造的总量相对减少了；城乡的住宅都失去了手艺的最后几笔——直到南北战争前夕，那几笔还零零星星地留存着。在开始填满大都市仍然偏僻郊区的那些笨拙乡村别墅里，风格感与比例感荡然无存：平面布满无意义的不规则；一种晦暗、泥泞的颜色漫上木质立面。有一部印制精美的大开本，收的是 1876 年的新港（Newport）别墅，我相信印数不过百册：编纂者想以此满足原主人的虚荣和后来一代的好奇；然而在所有这些「新奇」与「独特」的例证中间，却没有一座宅邸能满足任何可以想象的世世代代的后人。

===Original===
If the level of architecture was low in the country, it touched the bottom of the abyss in the city. As early as 1835 the multiple-family tenement had been introduced in New York as a means of producing congestion, raising the ground-rents, and satisfying in the worst possible way the need of the new immigrants for housing. The conditions of life in these tenements were infinitely lower than they had been in the most primitive farmhouse of the colonial period; their lack of light, lack of water, lack of sanitary facilities, and lack of privacy, created an admirable milieu for the propagation of vice and disease, and their existence in a period which was boasting loudly of the advance of science and industrialism shows, to say the least, how the myths which inspired the age stood between the eye and reality, and obscured the actual state of the modern industrial community.

To the disgrace of the architectural profession in America, the worst features of tenement-house construction were standardized in the so-called dumbbell tenement which won the first prize in the model tenement-house competition of 1879; and the tenements which were designed after this pattern in the succeeding years combined a maximum lack of privacy with a minimum of light and air. The gridiron street-design, the narrow frontage, the deep lot, all conspired to make good housing difficult in the larger cities: within this framework good house-design, indeed, still is difficult. The dumbbell tenement of the Gilded Age, however, raised bad housing into an art; and the acquisition of this art in its later developments is now one of the stigmata of “progress” in a modern American city. I say this without irony; the matter is too grave for jest.

During these same ’seventies, the benefits of poor housing were extended in New York to those with money enough to afford something better: the Paris flat was introduced. The legitimate excuse for the small apartment was the difficulty of obtaining household service, and the futility of keeping up large houses for small families: this, however, had nothing to do with the actual form that the apartment took, for, apart from the desire for congestion-rents, it is as easy to build apartments for two families as for twenty. The flat is a genuine convenience for the well-to-do visitor to a city; it gives him the atmosphere of a home without many of its major complications, and those who got the taste for this life in Paris were not altogether absurd in desiring to enjoy the same benefits in New York. Unfortunately, what suits a visitor does not necessarily meet the demands of a permanent resident: one may tolerate a blank wall for a week or a month without being depressed, particularly since a good part of a visitor’s time is spent outside his home; but to live year after year facing a blank wall or an equally-frowning façade opposite is to be condemned to the environment of a penitentiary.

The result of building apartments in New York and elsewhere was not cheaper rents for smaller quarters: it was smaller quarters without the cheap rents. Those who wanted sunlight and a pleasant view paid a premium for it; those who did not get either paid more than enough for what they got. The result of building apartments which would satisfy only a visitor was to make every family visitors: before the acute housing shortage, yearly removals to new premises were the only palliative that made their occupancy tolerable. The amount of wear and tear and waste, the loss of energy and money and good spirits, produced by the inability of the architect to design adequately under the pecuniary standards of the Gilded Age was colossal. The urban nomad in his own way was as great a spendthrift as the pioneer of the prairie. Both of them had been unable to create a permanent civilization; and both of them paid the price for it.

===Chinese===
如果说乡村的建筑水平低，那么城市的建筑则触到了深渊的底。早在 1835 年，多户合住的廉价公寓便作为一种手段被引入纽约，用以制造拥挤、抬高地面租金，并以最坏的方式满足新移民的住房需要。这些公寓里的生活条件，远低于殖民时期最简陋的农舍；缺光、缺水、缺少卫生设施、没有隐私，为罪恶与疾病的孳生造出了一个绝妙的温床；而它们存在于一个高声夸耀科学与工业主义之进步的时代，这至少说明，激励那个时代的种种神话如何挡在眼睛与现实之间，遮蔽了现代工业共同体的实际状况。

令美国建筑行业蒙羞的是，廉价公寓建造中最恶劣的特征，竟在所谓的哑铃式公寓（dumbbell tenement）中标准化了——它在 1879 年的模范廉价公寓设计竞赛中得了头奖；此后数年间照这个图式设计的公寓，把最大程度的无隐私与最小程度的光线和空气结合在一起。棋盘格的街道设计、窄面宽、深进深的地块，合谋使大城市里的良好住宅难乎为继：在这个框架里，良好的住宅设计到今天也确实仍然困难。然而镀金时代的哑铃式公寓把坏住宅提升成了一门艺术；这门艺术在日后的发展中的习得，如今已是现代美国城市「进步」的耻印记号之一。我说这话不带讽刺；这件事太严重，开不得玩笑。

同样在七十年代，糟糕住房的「好处」在纽约也推广到了那些出得起更好价钱的人：巴黎式公寓登场了。小公寓的正当理由，在于家务人手难求，在于小家庭撑不起大宅子：然而这与公寓实际采取的形式毫无关系，因为撇开对拥挤租金的渴望不谈，为两户建公寓与为二十户建公寓一样容易。对到城里做客的阔人，公寓是种真正的便利：它给他家的气氛，却没有家的种种大麻烦；在巴黎尝到这种生活滋味的人，想在纽约享受同样的好处，并非全然的荒唐。不幸的是，适合客居者的未必能满足常住者的要求：面对一堵光秃秃的墙，一个星期或一个月可以忍受而不消沉，尤其客居者的时间有大半消磨在家门之外；可是年复一年地面对一堵光秃的墙，或同样绷着脸的对面立面，就无异于被判处在监狱的环境里。

在纽约和别处建造公寓的结果，并不是小居室换来更便宜的租金：而是小居室照旧，便宜的租金却没有了。想要阳光和悦目景致的人，得为此付一笔溢价；两样都不占的人，为到手的那些也付了过头的钱。建造只合客居者心意的公寓，其结果是把每个家庭都变成了客居者：在尖锐的房荒到来之前，年年搬到新居，是使他们的居住勉强可忍的唯一缓解。建筑师在镀金时代的金钱标准之下无力于充分设计，由此造成的损耗与浪费、精力与金钱和心气的损失，是惊人的。城市里的游牧民，以他自己的方式，同草原上的拓荒者一样是大手大脚的挥霍者。两者都无力创造一种持久的文明；两者都为此付出了代价。

### III / 三

===Original===
During the first period of pioneering, mechanical improvements had affected the milieu of architecture, but not architecture itself, if one overlooks such ingenuities as the circular and octagon houses of the eighteen-thirties. Slowly, the actual methods of construction changed: the carpenter-builder, who had once performed every operation, gave way to the joiner, whose work profited by putty and paint, curtains and carpets﻿—to the plasterer, who covered up the raw imperfect frame﻿—and to the plumber. Weird ornamental forms for doors and window-architraves, for moldings and pendants, were supplied to the builder by the catalogs of the planing and scroll-saw mills. Invention produced novelties of contortion in wood, unique in ugliness and imbecile in design. Like the zinc and iron statues that graced the buildings of the Centennial Exposition, these devices record the absorption of art in a vain technology.

One need not dwell upon the results of all these miserable efforts, conceived in haste and aborted for profit: the phenomenon was common to industrial civilization at this period, and can be observed in Battersea and Manchester as well as in New York and Pittsburgh. Mr. Thomas Hardy, who was trained as an architect, wrote the esthetic apology for industrialism; and in proclaiming the rightness of our architectural deserts, one cannot help thinking that he transferred to the Wessex countryside a little of the horrible depression he must have acquired in London.

“Gay prospects,” exclaimed Mr. Hardy, “wed happily with gay times; but, alas! if the times be not gay! Men have more often suffered from the mockery of a place too smiling than from the oppression of surroundings oversadly tinged.﻿ ﻿… Indeed, it is a question if the exclusive reign of orthodox beauty is not approaching its last quarter. The new vale in Tempe may be a gaunt waste in Thule: human souls may find themselves in closer harmony with external things wearing a somberness distasteful to our race when it was young. Shall we say that man has grown so accustomed to his spiritual Bastille that he no longer looks forward to, and even shrinks from, a casual emergence into unusual brightness?”

Even the best work of the period is blighted with this sombreness: the fact that so many of Richardson’s buildings have the heavy air of a prison shows us that the Gilded Age was not, indeed, gay, and that a spiritual Black Friday perpetually threatened the calendar of its days.

===Chinese===
在拓殖的第一个时期，机械的改良影响着建筑的环境，却未触及建筑本身——如果不把三十年代那些圆形和八边形住宅的机巧算在内的话。营造的实际方法缓慢地改变了：昔日事必躬亲的木匠—建造者，让位给了细木工——他的活计靠油腻子与油漆、窗帘与地毯讨巧——让位给了抹灰工——他盖住毛糙不完美的构架——还让位给了管道工。门窗框线脚、模制线脚与吊坠那些古怪的样式，由刨木厂和线锯厂的图样册供给营造商。发明生产出木头拧麻花的种种新奇，丑得独一无二，设计得愚不可及。像装点百年纪念博览会（Centennial Exposition）建筑的锌像铁像一样，这些玩意记录下艺术被一种徒劳的技术吞并的过程。

这些可怜努力的后果——为赶工而怀胎、为牟利而流产——无须多论：这现象是这一时期工业文明所共有的，在巴特西（Battersea）和曼彻斯特（Manchester）同在纽约和匹兹堡一样见得到。托马斯·哈代（Thomas Hardy）先生受过建筑师的训练，他为工业主义写下了审美上的辩词；在宣告我们建筑荒漠的正当性时，人们不禁想到，他把他在伦敦必定染上的那种可怕的抑郁，挪了一点到威塞克斯（Wessex）的乡野上。

「明朗的前景，」哈代先生叹道，「与明朗的时代喜结良缘；可是，唉！倘若时代不明朗呢！人们因一处过分明媚的景致的嘲弄而受的苦，往往多于因四周过分阴郁的笼罩而受的压迫。……的确，正统之美的一统天下是否正在接近它的最后一刻，这已成问题。坦佩的新谷到了图勒也许是荒瘠的废墟：人的灵魂也许会发现自己与外物更为契合，当外物披上一种我们的种族在年轻时不曾欢喜的晦暗。我们是否可以说，人已经如此习惯于他的精神的巴士底狱，以致他不再期盼、甚至畏缩于偶然浮现的异常光明？」

这个时期连最好的作品也染上了这种阴沉：理查森那么多建筑都带着监狱般的滞重气息，这一事实向我们表明，镀金时代实在谈不上明朗，一场精神上的黑色星期五（Black Friday）永远威胁着它的日历。

### IV / 四

===Original===
If the romantic movement in America proved that the architect could capture only a small part of the field, and go no further than the interests of privilege allowed, the building of the Brooklyn Bridge showed how well industrialism could handle its problems when its purposes were not limited by the necessity for sloppy workmanship and quick turnover. The story of its building is a tribute to both science and humanity. When John Roebling, the designer of the bridge, died in the midst of his job, the business of construction was taken up by his son, and by his devotion to his task in season and out of season, Washington Roebling became an invalid. Confined to his house on Columbia Heights, for ten years the younger Roebling watched the work through a telescope, and directed it as a general would direct a battle. So goes the legend: it runs rather higher than the tales of mean prudence or mechanical skill which glorified Mr. Samuel Smiles’ heroes.

The bridge itself was a testimony to the swift progress of physical science. The strong lines of the bridge, and the beautiful curve described by its suspended cables, were derived from an elegant formula in mathematical physics﻿—the elastic curve. If the architectural elements of the massive piers have perhaps too much the bare quality of engineering, if the pointed arches meet esthetic betrayal in the flat solidity of the cornices, if, in short, the masonry does not sing as Richardson alone perhaps could have made it sing, the steel work itself makes up for this, by the architectural beauty of its pattern; so that beyond any other aspect of New York, I think, the Brooklyn Bridge has been a source of joy and inspiration to the artist. In the later bridges the spanning members are sturdier and the supporting piers and cables are lighter and less essential; and they suffer esthetically by the very ease of their triumph over the difficulties of engineering.

All that the age had just cause for pride in﻿—its advances in science, its skill in handling iron, its personal heroism in the face of dangerous industrial processes, its willingness to attempt the untried and the impossible﻿—came to a head in the Brooklyn Bridge. What was grotesque and barbarous in industrialism was sloughed off in the great bridges. These avenues of communication are, paradoxically, the only enduring monuments that witness a period of uneasy industrial transition; and to this day they communicate a feeling of dignity, stability, and unwavering poise.

===Chinese===
如果说美国的浪漫主义运动证明，建筑师只能夺取这个领域的一小部分，而且走不出特权利益所容许的范围，那么布鲁克林大桥（Brooklyn Bridge）的建造则表明，当工业主义的目的不受草率施工与快速周转的必需所限时，它能把自己的问题处理得多么好。它的建造故事是对科学与人性的双重礼赞。大桥的设计者约翰·罗布林（John Roebling）殉职于工程中途，建造的事业由他的儿子接手；由于不分寒暑地献身于这项任务，华盛顿·罗布林（Washington Roebling）成了残废之人。困在哥伦比亚高地（Columbia Heights）的寓所里，小罗布林用望远镜注视了工程十年，像将军指挥一场战役那样指挥它。传说如此流传：它比起那些颂扬塞缪尔·斯迈尔斯（Samuel Smiles）先生笔下英雄的吝啬谨慎或机械技艺的故事，格调要高出一截。

大桥本身就是物理科学迅猛进步的见证。桥的遒劲线条，悬索描出的美妙曲线，得自数学物理里一个优美的公式——弹性曲线（elastic curve）。如果说厚重桥墩的建筑元素或许带着太多赤裸裸的工程气质，如果说尖拱在檐口的平板厚重中遭到审美的背叛，如果说，简言之，这石工唱不出歌——大概只有理查森能让它唱——钢构件本身却补足了这一点，以它图式的建筑之美；因此，超出纽约的任何其他方面，我认为布鲁克林大桥一直是艺术家的欢愉与灵感之源。在后来的桥梁上，跨越构件更粗壮，支撑的墩与索更轻、更不紧要；它们恰恰因战胜工程难题的轻而易举而在审美上受损。

这个时代一切有正当理由引以为傲的东西——它科学的进步，它驾驭钢铁的技艺，它面对危险工业过程时个人的英勇，它尝试未试过与不可能之事的意愿——都在布鲁克林大桥上达到顶点。工业主义中怪诞而野蛮的部分，在大桥身上蜕落了。悖论的是，这些大道通途是见证那段不安的工业过渡期的唯一不朽纪念碑；直到今天，它们仍传达出一种尊严、稳定与从容不迫的沉着。

===Original===
The Brooklyn Bridge was opened in 1884; Richardson died, after finishing the Pittsburgh Court House, in 1886. There was a short period during which the echoes of Richardson’s style resounded in the work of the Western architects; and then in New York two of Richardson’s own pupils, Messrs. McKim and White, who had caught the spirit of the period that was to follow the passing of the frontier, prepared an appropriate mold for its activities. By far the finest things in the late ’eighties are the shingled houses which Richardson and Stanford White and a few others developed for seaboard estates: they recovered the spirit of the early vernacular work, and continued the colonial tradition without even faintly recalling colonial forms. This new note, however, was scarcely sounded before it died out; and in the twenty years that followed the conflict between industrialism and romanticism was swallowed up and finally forgotten in the rise of a new mode. Richardson had not died too soon. The quality of mind and culture which shines through his work was opposed to nearly every manifestation of the period that succeeded him.

From this time on, romanticism retained a place for itself only by forfeiting its claims to occupy the whole province of architecture. In churches and college halls where the traditional tie with the Middle Ages had never perhaps been completely broken, its triumphs have been genuine; but although Mr. J. G. Rogers’ Harkness Memorial at Yale, or Messrs. Goodhue and Cram’s St. Thomas’ Church, for example, leave little to be desired in themselves, they have established no precedent for the hundred other kinds of building which the modern community requires; and it is not without significance that in his most recent efforts Mr. Goodhue, for one, had abandoned the molds of romanticism. Unlike Richardson, the surviving romanticists now demand a certain insulation from the modern world; the more intelligent exponents of the movement believe with Dr. Ralph Adams Cram that there is no hope for its achievement throughout the community without a return to “Walled Towns.”

Such a retreat is the equivalent of surrender. To hold to Gothic precedent in the hope of recreating the medieval community is to hope that an ancient bottle will turn potassium permanganate into claret. The romanticists have never fully faced the social and economic problems that attend their architectural solutions: the result is that they have been dependent upon assistance from the very forces and institutions which, fundamentally, they aim to combat. Isolated on little islands, secure for the moment, romanticism must view the work on the mainland with a gesture of irate despair; and the only future it dares to face lies behind it!

===Chinese===
布鲁克林大桥于 1884 年通车；理查森在完成匹兹堡法院大楼之后，于 1886 年去世。有一小段时期，理查森风格的余音回响在西部建筑师的作品里；随后在纽约，理查森自己的两个学生——麦金先生与怀特先生（Messrs. McKim and White）——他们已抓住了边陲消逝之后那个时代的精神，为它的种种活动备好了一个合式的模子。八十年代晚期最好的东西，远非其他，而是理查森与斯坦福·怀特（Stanford White）和另外几人为滨海庄园发展的鱼鳞板木瓦住宅：它们找回了早期乡土作品的精神，延续了殖民地的传统，却连殖民地的形式都丝毫不曾唤起。然而这个新声几乎没有响起便消逝了；在其后的二十年里，工业主义与浪漫主义的冲突被一种新模式的兴起吞没，终至遗忘。理查森没有死得太早。闪耀在他的作品中的心智与文化的品质，几乎同他身后那个时代的每一种表现都相抵触。

从这时起，浪漫主义只有放弃占据整个建筑疆域的诉求，才为自己保住了一席之地。在那些与中世纪的传统纽带或许从未完全断绝的教堂和学院讲堂里，它的成功是真实的；但是，尽管耶鲁的 J·G·罗杰斯（J. G. Rogers）先生的哈克尼斯纪念馆（Harkness Memorial），或古德休与克拉姆（Goodhue and Cram）两位先生的圣托马斯教堂（St. Thomas’ Church）本身已无可挑剔，它们却没有为现代共同体所需的其他百种建筑树立任何先例；而且不无深意的是，古德休先生在其最后的努力中，已经抛弃了浪漫主义的模子。与理查森不同，幸存的浪漫主义者如今要求与现代世界保持某种隔绝；这场运动中较有见识的鼓吹者与拉尔夫·亚当斯·克拉姆（Ralph Adams Cram）博士一样相信，若不回到「围城」（Walled Towns），便无望在整个共同体中实现其理想。

这样的退守无异于投降。抱着再创中世纪共同体的希望而坚守哥特式先例，无异于指望一只古瓶把高锰酸钾变成红葡萄酒。浪漫主义者从未完全正视伴随其建筑方案而来的社会与经济问题：结果，他们一直依赖的，恰恰是那些他们根本上想对抗的力量与制度的援助。孤悬在小岛上，一时安稳，浪漫主义只能以怒而至绝望的手势眺望大陆上的工程；而它敢于面对的唯一未来，在它身后！

## IV The Diaspora of the Pioneer / 拓荒者的离散



### I / 一



===Original===
From the standpoint of architecture, the early part of the nineteenth century was a period of disintegration. The gap between sheer utility and art, which the Renaissance had emphasized, was widened with the coming of machinery. That part of architecture which was touched by industrialism became crude beyond belief: the new mills and factories were usually packing boxes, lacking in light and ventilation, and the homes of the factory workers, when they were not the emptied houses of merchants and tradesmen, made to serve a dozen families instead of the original one, were little more than covered pens, as crowded as a cattle market. At the same time that the old forms were undermined by the new methods of mechanical production, a sentimental longing to retain those forms, just because they were old, seized men’s minds; and so industrialism and romanticism divided the field of architecture between them.

It was no accident that caused romanticism and industrialism to appear at the same time. They were rather the two faces of the new civilization, one looking towards the past, and the other towards the future; one glorifying the new, the other clinging to the old; industrialism intent on increasing the physical means of subsistence, romanticism living in a sickly fashion on the hollow glamour of the past. The age not merely presented these two aspects; it sought to enjoy each of them. Where industrialism took root, the traditions of architecture were disregarded; where romanticism flourished, on the other hand, in the mansions, public buildings, and churches, architecture became capricious and absurd, and it returned to a past that had never existed. Against the gross callousness which a Bounderby exhibited toward beauty and amenity, there was only the bland piety of a Pecksniff.

The dream that is dying and the dream that is coming to birth do not stand in sequence, but mingle as do the images in a dissolving view; and during the very years that the architecture of the Renaissance, both in Europe and America, achieved new heights of formal design, the first factories were being planted in Staffordshire and Yorkshire, the Duke of Bridgewater built his famous canal, and Horace Walpole designed his “Gothic” mansion on Strawberry Hill. The coincidence of industrialism and romanticism is just as emphatic in America as in England; and it is not without historic justice that the architect who in 1807 designed the chapel of St. Mary’s Seminary in Baltimore, after the Gothic fashion, successfully introduced a steam pumping system in Philadelphia’s waterworks. While the industrial buildings of the period represented nothing but a lapse from the current vernacular, due to haste and insufficient resources, romantic architecture was a positive influence; and it will perhaps best serve our purpose to examine the romantic heritage in its pristine form, rather than in the work of disciples like Latrobe, whose American practice is dated about two generations later.

===Chinese===
从建筑的角度看，十九世纪早期是一个解体的时期。文艺复兴所凸显的纯实用与艺术之间的鸿沟，随着机器的到来而加宽了。建筑中受工业主义触及的那部分，变得粗陋得难以置信：新的磨坊和工厂通常都是包装箱，缺光少风；工厂工人的住宅，若不是被腾空的商贾铺宅改作他用——原供一户住的房子塞进了一打人家——便几乎无异于带顶的围栏，拥挤得如同牲口市场。就在旧形式被机械生产的新方法掏空根基的同时，一种纯粹因为它们老旧而要将它们保留下来的伤感渴望，攫住了人们的心智；于是工业主义与浪漫主义瓜分了建筑的疆场。

浪漫主义与工业主义同时出现，并非偶然。它们毋宁说是新文明的两个面孔：一个望向过去，一个望向未来；一个颂扬新的，一个死抱旧的；工业主义一心扩充维生的物质手段，浪漫主义则以病态的方式靠往昔空洞的光艳过活。这个时代不只是呈现出这两个方面；它还试图两头兼享。在工业主义扎根的地方，建筑的传统无人理会；而在浪漫主义盛行的宅邸、公共建筑和教堂里，建筑则变得任诞而荒唐，退回到一个从未存在过的过去。面对邦德比（Bounderby）那种对美与舒适的全然麻木，人们手里只有佩克斯尼夫（Pecksniff）式的温和虔敬。

正在死去的梦与正在出世的梦并不依序排列，而是像渐隐画面里的影像那样交叠混合；恰恰在文艺复兴建筑在欧洲和美洲双双攀上形式设计新高的那些年月里，第一批工厂在斯塔福德郡和约克郡拔地而起，布里奇沃特公爵开凿了他那条著名的运河，霍勒斯·沃波尔（Horace Walpole）在草莓山设计了他的「哥特式」宅邸。工业主义与浪漫主义的重合，在美国一如在英国那样醒目；而那位 1807 年仿哥特式样设计巴尔的摩圣玛丽神学院（St. Mary's Seminary）小教堂的建筑师，正是成功地把蒸汽抽水系统引进费城自来水厂的人——此中史实，可谓恰如其分。这一时期的工业建筑，由于仓促和资源不足，不过是当时乡土营造的一次跌落；浪漫建筑却是一种积极的影响；因此，考察浪漫主义的遗产，与其取诸拉特罗布（Latrobe）等约两代之后才在美国开业的后学之作，不如取其本来面目，或许最能切合我们的目的。

===Original===
The author of *The Castle of Otranto* had a perverse and wayward interest in the past; and the spirit he exhibited in both his novel and his country home was typical of the romantic attitude everywhere. What attracted Walpole to the Gothic style was little more than the phosphorescence of decay: he summoned up the ghosts of the Middle Ages but not the guilds; and instead of admiring the soundness of medieval masonry, those who followed directly in his path were affected rather by the spectacle of its dilapidation, so that the production of authentic ruins became one of the chief efforts of the eighteenth-century landscape gardener.

It is not a great step from building a ruin to building a mansion that is little better than a ruin. While Walpole defended Strawberry Hill by saying he did not aim to make his house so Gothic as to exclude convenience, it happened again and again that the picturesque was the enemy of simple honesty and necessity; and just as Walpole himself in his refectory used wall paper that imitated stucco, so did other owners and builders use plaster and hangings and wall paper and carpet to cover up defects of construction. Towers that no one ever climbed, turrets that no one could enter, and battlements that no one rose to defend, took the place of the classic orders. The drawbridge-and-moat that embellished Mr. Wemmick’s villa in *Great Expectations* was not a wild conceit of Dickens but a relic of Walpole and his successors.

As a disguise for mean or thoughtless workmanship, the application of antique “style” was the romantic contribution to architecture; and it served very handily during the period of speculative building and selling that accompanied the growth of the new industrial towns. Even where style did not conceal commercial disingenuousness, it covered up a poverty of imagination in handling the elements of a building. Gothic touches about doors and the exterior of windows, and a heap of bric-a-brac and curios on the inside, softened the gauntness and bareness of this architecture, or rather, distracted attention from them. Curiosity was the dominant mood of the time, acquisitiveness its principal impulse, and comfort its end. Many good things doubtless came out of this situation; but architecture was not one of them.

===Chinese===
《奥特兰托城堡》（*The Castle of Otranto*）的作者对过去怀着一种执拗任性的兴趣；他在小说和乡间宅邸里表现出来的精神，正是各地浪漫态度的典型。吸引沃波尔走向哥特式的，多半只是朽败的磷光：他召唤出中世纪的幽灵，却没有召唤出中世纪的行会；直承他衣钵的人们，感服的不是中世纪砌体的坚实，而是其倾颓的景观，于是，造出逼真的废墟，便成了十八世纪造园家的一大功课。

从建造一座废墟到建造一座比废墟好不了多少的豪宅，其间并无多大一步。沃波尔为草莓山辩护时说，他并不想把宅子弄得哥特到排斥便利的地步；然而如画之美一再成为质朴诚实与实际必需之敌；正如沃波尔本人在饭厅里用仿灰泥的墙纸，别的业主和建造者也用抹灰、帷幔、墙纸和地毯来掩盖施工的缺陷。无人攀登的塔楼、进不去的角楼、无人起身捍卫的雉堞，取代了古典柱式。《远大前程》（*Great Expectations*）里装点韦米克先生（Mr. Wemmick）别墅的吊桥与壕沟，并非狄更斯的狂想，而是沃波尔及其后学的遗物。

古式「风格」的敷施，是浪漫主义对建筑的贡献，用来遮掩寒伧或漫不经心的手艺；在新工业城镇成长所伴随的投机建造与投机出售的年代，它服务得极为便利。即便风格没有掩盖商业上的不老实，它也掩盖了处理建筑元素时想象力的贫乏。门窗外缘的哥特式点缀，屋内一堆古玩摆设，柔化了这种建筑的嶙峋与光秃——或者不如说，转移了人们对它们的注意。好奇是那个时代的主导情绪，占有是它的主要冲动，舒适是它的归宿。许多好东西无疑从这个局面中产生；建筑却不在其列。
### II / 二



===Original===
Modern industrialism began to take root in America after the War of Independence, and its effect was twofold: it started up new villages which centered about the waterfall or the iron mine and had scarcely any other concern than industry; at the same time, by cutting canals which tapped the interior, it drew life away from the smaller provincial ports and concentrated commerce and population in great towns like Boston, Philadelphia, and New York. In New England, as in the English Cotswolds from Whitney to Chalford, the mechanical regime was humanized by the presence of an older civilization, and the first generation of factory hands were farmers’ lads and lasses who neither lost nor endangered their independence; but where the factory depended upon paupers or immigrants, as it did in the big towns and in some of the unsettled parts of the country, the community relapsed into a barbarism which affected the masters as well as the hands. There was more than a difference in literary taste between the Corinths and Bethels named by an earlier generation and the Mechanicsvilles that followed them.

The chief watchwords of the time were progress and expansion. The first belonged to the pioneer in industry who opened up new areas for mechanical invention and applied science; the second, to the land pioneer; and between these two resourceful types the old ways, were they good or bad, were scrapped, and the new ways, were they good or bad, were adopted. Both land pioneering and industrial pioneering were essentially subdivisions of one occupation, mining; and, following the clue opened by Messrs. Geddes and Branford, one may say with Professor Adshead that the nineteenth century witnessed “the great attack of the miner on the peasant.”

Mechanical industry owes its great development and progress to the work of the woodman and the miner: the first type of worker takes the bent sapling and develops the lathe or “bodger” which is still to be found in the remote parts of the Chiltern Hills in England, while from the mine itself not merely comes the steam engine, first used for pumping out water, but likewise the railway. The perpetual debris amid which the miner lives forms a capital contrast with the ordered culture, the careful weeding and cutting, of field and orchard: almost any sort of habitation is an advance upon the squalor of the pithead; and it is not a mere chance that the era devoted to mining and all its accessory manufactures was throughout the western world the dingiest and dirtiest that has yet befouled the earth. Choked by his own debris, or stirred by the exhaustion of minerals, the miner’s community runs down﻿—and he departs.

===Chinese===
现代工业主义在独立战争之后开始在美国扎根，其效果有二：它兴起了以瀑布或铁矿为中心、除工业外几乎别无牵挂的新村庄；同时，又以凿通内地的运河，抽走了较小地方港口的生气，把商业和人口集中到波士顿、费城、纽约一类的大城市。在新英格兰，正如在英国科茨沃尔德从惠特尼到查尔福德一带，机械体制因旧有文明的存在而显得人情化，第一代工厂工人是农家儿女，他们的自立既未丧失也未受损；但在工厂依靠贫民或移民的地方——大城市里和国内某些未开发地区即是如此——共同体便退堕回一种野蛮状态，老板和工人同受感染。上一代人所命名的那些科林斯们（Corinths）与贝塞尔们（Bethels），同随后而来的机械村们（Mechanicsvilles）之间，其差别远不止在文学趣味上。

那个时代的主要口号是进步与扩张。前者属于工业里的拓荒者，他们为机械发明和应用科学开辟新天地；后者属于土地上的拓荒者；在这两类足智多谋的人之间，旧办法无论好坏一概废弃，新办法无论好坏一概采用。土地拓荒与工业拓荒，本质上都是同一种营生的分支——采矿；循着格迪斯（Geddes）与布兰福德（Branford）二位先生开启的线索，我们可以跟着阿兹黑德教授（Adshead）说：十九世纪目睹了「矿工对农夫的大进攻」。

机械工业的巨大发展与进步，应归功于伐木人和矿工的劳作：前一类工人从弯折的树苗发展出车床，或曰「旋工凳」（bodger）——在英格兰奇尔特恩丘陵腹地至今犹可见到；而从矿井本身，不仅产生了蒸汽机——最初用于抽水——同样也产生了铁路。矿工栖身其间的那经年累月的废渣堆，同田野与果园的整然耕作、细心刈剪，构成了一流对照：几乎任何一种居所，比起井口边的污秽都是一种进步；而献身于采矿及其一切附属制造业的那个时代，在整个西方世界都是迄今玷污过大地的最昏暗最肮脏的时代，这也绝非偶然。被自己的废渣噎住，或被矿藏的枯竭所驱策，矿工的社区便衰败下去——而他一走了之。

===Original===
The name pioneer has a romantic color; but in America the land pioneer mined the forests and the soil, and the industry pioneer almost as ruthlessly mined the human resources, and when the pay-dirt got sallow and thin, they both moved on. Longfellow’s allusion to the “bivouac of life” unconsciously points to the prevailing temper; for even those who remained in the older American centers were affected by the pioneer’s malaise and unsettlement; and they behaved as if at any moment they might be called to the colors and sent westward.

Beside the vivid promises of Mechanical Progress and Manifest Destiny the realities of an ordered society thinned into a pale vapor. In many little communities Mechanical Societies were formed for the propagation of the utilitarian faith: industrialism with its ascetic ritual of unsparing work, its practice of thrift, its renunciation of the arts, gathered to itself the religious zeal of Protestantism. The erection of factories, the digging of canals, the location of furnaces, the building of roads, the devising of inventions, not merely exhausted a great part of the available capital; even more, it occupied the energy and imagination of the more vigorous spirits. Two generations before, Thomas Jefferson could lay out and develop the estate of Monticello; now, with many of Jefferson’s capacities, Poe could only dream about the fantastic Domain of Arnheim. The society around Poe had no more use for an architectural imagination than the Puritans had for decorative images; the smoke of the factory-chimney was incense, the scars on the landscape were as the lacerations of a saint, and the mere multiplication of gaunt sheds and barracks was a sign of progress, and therefore an earnest of perfection.

Did ever so many elements of disintegration come together at one time and place before? The absence of tradition and example raised enough difficulties in Birmingham and Manchester and Lyons and Essen; but in America it was accentuated by the restless march of those pioneers who, in the words of a contemporary economist, “leave laws, education and the arts, all the essential elements of civilization, behind them.” What place could architecture fill in these squatter communities? It could diminish the hardships of living; it could grease the channels of gain; and it could demolish or “improve” so much of the old as it could not understand, as Bulfinch’s Court House in Newburyport was improved, and as many a fine city residence was swept away under the tide of traffic.

===Chinese===
拓荒者之名带有浪漫色彩；但在美国，土地拓荒者采掘的是森林与土壤，工业拓荒者几乎同样无情地采掘的是人力资源，一旦含金的矿层变得贫薄，两路人马便都开拔了。朗费罗（Longfellow）对「人生宿营地」的暗用，无意间点出了弥漫一时的气质：因为连留在美国旧中心的人，也感染了拓荒者的躁郁与不安定；他们的举止，仿佛随时会被征召入伍、派往西方。

在机械进步与昭昭天命的鲜活许诺旁边，一个秩序井然的社会的种种现实，稀薄成了一缕惨白的水汽。在许多小共同体里，成立了传播功利信仰的机械协会：工业主义连同它那不惜工本的苦行仪轨、它的节俭实践、它对艺术的弃绝，把新教的宗教热忱收归己有。建造工厂、开凿运河、选定熔炉位置、修筑道路、设计发明，不仅耗尽了可用资本的一大部分；更甚者，它占用了较有活力者的精力与想象。两代人之前，托马斯·杰斐逊（Thomas Jefferson）还能规划并营建蒙蒂塞洛庄园；如今，兼具杰斐逊诸多才具的爱伦·坡（Poe），却只能梦想那奇幻的阿恩海姆领地（Domain of Arnheim）。坡周围的社会对建筑想象力的需要，并不比清教徒对装饰圣像的需要更多；工厂烟囱的烟是馨香，大地景观上的疤痕是圣徒的鞭痕，嶙峋棚屋与营房的单纯增殖便是进步的标志，因而也是臻于至善的凭证。

如此之多的解体要素，可曾在此之前于同一时间、同一地点聚齐过？传统与先例的阙如，在伯明翰、曼彻斯特、里昂和埃森已经造成够多的困难；而在美国，这困难又因拓荒者们不息的进军而变本加厉——用一位同时代经济学家的话说，他们「把法律、教育和艺术，把文明的一切基本要素，都抛在了身后」。建筑在这些拓居者的共同体里能占什么位置？它能减轻生活的艰苦；它能给牟利的渠道上油；它还能把它理解不了的旧物加以拆除或「改良」，一如布尔芬奇（Bulfinch）在新伯里港的法院所遭受的改良，一如许多精美的城市宅邸被交通的洪流冲走。
===Original===
These were the days when the log cabin flourished; but it did not remain long enough in place to become the well-wrought and decorative piece of rustic architecture that the better sort of peasant hut, done with the same materials, became in Russia. A genuine architectural development might have led from a crude log cabin to a finished one, from a bare cabin to an enriched and garnished one, and so, perhaps, in the course of a century or so, to a fine country architecture and a great native art of wood carving comparable to that of the Russian sculptors today. In America, however, the pioneer jumped baldly from log cabin to White House, or its genteel and scroll-sawed equivalent; and the arts inherent in good building never had a chance to develop. With the animus of the miner in back of everything the pioneer attempted, the pioneer’s architecture was all false-work and scantling.

===Chinese===
那正是圆木小屋兴盛的年月；但它在一地停留得不够久，未能像俄国较好的农舍那样，用同样的材料，打磨成一件做工考究、富于装饰的乡土建筑。一种真正的建筑发展，本可以从粗陋的圆木屋走到完善的圆木屋，从素朴的小屋走到丰赡修饰的小屋，这样，或许在一个世纪左右的时间里，走到一种精美的乡村建筑和一门伟大的本土木雕艺术，可与今日俄国的雕塑家比肩。然而在美国，拓荒者从圆木小屋一跃而跳到白宫，或其气派而线锯镂花的等价物；良好营造固有的技艺从未得到发展的机会。由于拓荒者尝试的一切背后都藏着矿工的心性，拓荒者的建筑便尽是临时撑架和零星木料。

### III / 三

===Original===
The first contribution to the pioneer’s comfort was Franklin’s ingenious stove (1745). After that came a number of material appliances. Central heating gave the American house a Roman standard of comfort, the astral-oil lamp captivated Edgar Poe; and cooking stoves, gas-lighting, permanent bathtubs, and water-closets made their way into the better sort of house in the Eastern cities before the middle of the nineteenth century. In the development of the city itself, the gridiron plan was added to the list of laborsaving devices. Although the gridiron plan had the same relation to natural conditions and fundamental social needs as a paper constitution has to the living customs of a people, the simplicity of the gridiron plan won the heart of the pioneer. Its rectangular blocks formed parcels of land which he could sell by the front foot and gamble with as easily as if he were playing cards, and deeds of transfer could be drawn up hastily with the same formula for each plot; moreover, the least competent surveyor, without thought or knowledge, could project the growth of New Eden’s streets and avenues into an interminable future. In nineteenth-century city planning the engineer was the willing servant of the land monopolist; and he provided a frame for the architect﻿—a frame in which we still struggle today﻿—where site-value counted for everything, and sight-value was not even an afterthought.

In street layout and land subdivision no attention was paid to the final use to which the land would be put; but the most meticulous efforts were made to safeguard its immediate use, namely, land-speculation. In order to further this use hills were graded, swamps and ponds filled, and streets laid out before these expenditures could be borne by the people who, in the end, were to profit by or suffer from them. It was no wonder that the newer towns like Cincinnati, St. Louis, and Chicago by the middle of the century had forfeited to the gambler in real estate, to pay the cost of street improvements, generous tracts of land which the original planners had set aside as civic centers. Planned by men who still retained some of the civic vision of the early republic, the commercial city speedily drifted into the hands of people who had no more civic scruples than the keeper of a lottery.

The gridiron plan had one other defect which was accounted a virtue by the pioneer, and still is shared by those who have not profited by the intervening century’s experience. With its avenues that encompassed swamps and wildernesses, with its future growth forecast for at least a hundred years, the complete city plan captivated the imagination. Scarcely any American town was so mean that it did not attempt to grow faster than its neighbor, faster perhaps than New York. Only by the accumulation of more and more people could its colossal city plan and its inflated land values be realized. If the older cities of the seaboard were limited in their attempts to become metropolises by the fact that their downtown sections were originally laid out for villages, the villages of the middle west labored under just the opposite handicap; they had frequently acquired the framework of a metropolis before they had passed out of the physical state of a village. The gridiron plan was a sort of hand-me-down which the juvenile city was supposed to grow into and fill. That a city had any other purpose than to attract trade, to increase land values, and to grow is something that, if it uneasily entered the mind of an occasional Whitman, never exercised any hold upon the minds of the majority of our countrymen. For them, the place where the great city stands *is* the place of stretched wharves, and markets, and ships bringing goods from the ends of the earth; that, and nothing else.

===Chinese===
对拓荒者舒适的第一项贡献，是富兰克林那精巧的火炉（1745）。此后便有了一连串物质器械。集中供暖给了美国住宅以罗马标准的舒适，星油灯迷住了爱伦·坡；炉灶、煤气灯、固定浴缸和抽水马桶，在十九世纪中叶之前便已进入东部城市中较好的人家。在城市自身的发展中，网格规划被添进了省力器械的清单。尽管网格规划与自然条件和基本社会需求的关系，恰如一纸成文宪法与一个民族活的风俗的关系，网格规划的简单却赢得了拓荒者的心。它的矩形街块形成了地块，他可以按临街尺码出售，可以拿来做赌注，就像玩纸牌一样便当；每块地的产权转移文书都可以仓促间用同一套公式拟就；况且，最不济的测量员，不假思索、不加调查，也能把新伊甸的街道与大街的增长投射进无穷的未来。在十九世纪的城市规划中，工程师是土地垄断者心甘情愿的仆人；他为建筑师提供了一个框架——一个我们至今仍在其中挣扎的框架——在这里，地段价值就是一切，景观价值连事后想起的资格都没有。

在街道布局和土地划分上，没有人留意土地最终的用途；但为保障其即时用途——即土地投机——却用尽了最细致的功夫。为促成这一用途，山丘被削平，沼泽和池塘被填没，街道被划出，而这一切开销，还轮不到最终将从中获利或受害的人民来承担。无怪乎到中叶，辛辛那提、圣路易斯和芝加哥一类的新兴城市，已经把最初规划者留作市政中心的大片土地，输给了地产赌客，以支付街道改良的费用。这些城市出自仍保有早期共和国几分市政远见的人的手笔，却迅速落入一帮人的手中，他们的市政顾忌，不比彩票庄家更多。

网格规划还有另一重缺陷，拓荒者却把它当作美德；此后一百年间的经验未能使之受益的人，至今仍与它共享这个缺陷。它的大道圈进沼泽与荒野，它的未来增长被预报了至少一百年，这样一份完整的城市规划叫人心醉神迷。几乎没有一个美国市镇卑微到不想长得比邻镇更快，或许比纽约更快。唯有靠越来越多的人口的积聚，它那庞大的城市规划和它那膨胀的地价才能兑现。如果说沿海的老城市因其市中心最初按村庄规划而受限于都会化之图，中西部诸村却苦于恰恰相反的障碍：它们往往在尚未脱去村庄的形体之前，就已经取得了大都会的骨架。网格规划是一件传下来的旧衣裳，幼稚的城市理应长进去、撑满它。城市除了招徕生意、抬高地价、不断长大之外还有别的目的——这个念头，纵使偶尔不安地掠过某一位惠特曼（Whitman）的心头，也从未在我们的多数同胞心里占得住脚。在他们看来，大城市所在之处，*就是*码头绵延、市场罗列、海船从天涯海角载货而来的地方；仅此而已，别无其他。
### IV / 四



===Original===
With business booming and vanishing, with people coming and going, with land continually changing hands, what encouragement was there for the stable achievements of architecture? In vain does the architect antic and grimace to conceal his despair; his business is to put on a front. If he is not a Pecksniff at heart, he will at any rate have to serve Mr. Veneering. A guide book of 1826 refers to a Masonic Hall “somewhat in the Gothic style”; and we can characterize all the buildings of the period by saying that they were “somewhat” like architecture﻿—a little more than scenery, a little less than solids.

For a while it seemed as if the Gothic revival might give the prevailing cast to nineteenth-century building; for if this mode was adopted at first because it was picturesque and historic it was later reinforced by the conviction that it was a natural and scientific mode of construction, that it stood for growth and function, as against the arbitrary character of the classic work. The symbols of the organic world were rife in the thought of this period, for in the sphere of thought biology was supplanting physics, and Gothic architecture was supposed peculiarly to be in the line of growth, while that of the Renaissance cut across and, heretically, denied the principle of organic development. Unfortunately the process of disintegration had gone so far that no one current of thought had the power to dominate; and the Gothic style proved to be only the first of a number of discordant influences, derived from industry, from history, from archaeology.

Indeed, the chief sign that bears witness to the disintegration of architecture during the formative days of the pioneer is eclecticism; but there is still another﻿—the attempt to justify the industrial process by using solely the materials it had created in abundance. In discussing the plans for the Smithsonian Institution, Robert Dale Owen observed that “of late years a rival material, from the mine, seems encroaching on these [stone, clay, wood] and the next generation may see, arising on our continent, villages, or it may be cities, of iron.”

===Chinese===
生意时而爆响时而蒸发，人群川流不息，土地不断易手，建筑那沉得住气的成就还能得到什么鼓励呢？建筑师装腔作势、挤眉弄眼，想遮住自己的绝望，终归徒劳；他的本行就是装个门面。他心里纵使不是佩克斯尼夫，至少也得伺候维尼林先生（Mr. Veneering）。一本 1826 年的指南提到一座共济会堂「略带哥特式风味」；我们可以借此给这个时期的所有建筑下一考语：它们「略似」建筑——比布景多一点，比实体少一点。

有一阵子，哥特复兴似乎要给十九世纪的营造定下主导的色调；因为这一样式起初之所以被采纳，是由于它如画而有古意，后来又得到一种信念的加固：它是一种自然而科学的建造方式，它代表生长与功能，与古典作品的武断性格相对。有机世界的象征充斥于这一时期的思想，因为在思想领域里，生物学正在取代物理学；哥特建筑被认定格外合于生长之脉，而文艺复兴的建筑则横截其道，异端般地否认有机发展的原则。不幸的是，解体的进程已经走得太远，没有哪一股思想潮流还拥有主宰的力量；而哥特式也终被证明只是一连串不协和影响中的头一个——这些影响，有的来自工业，有的来自历史，有的来自考古学。

的确，在拓荒者的成形年代里，见证建筑解体的头号标志是折衷主义；但还有另一个标志——试图单用工业流程大量造出的材料，来为这一流程正名。在讨论史密森学会（Smithsonian Institution）的方案时，罗伯特·戴尔·欧文（Robert Dale Owen）评说道：「近些年来，一种来自矿山的敌手材料，似乎正在进逼这些材料〔石、黏土、木材〕；下一代也许会看到，在我们这片大陆上，耸起一座座铁的村庄，或者铁的城市。」

===Original===
What Owen’s generation actually did see, apart from sheet-iron façades and zinc cornices, was the Crystal Palace, which was built in New York in 1853 in imitation of London’s exhibition hall of 1850. Ruskin described the original Crystal Palace, with sardonic justice, as a magnified conservatory; and that is about all that can be said for either building. As exercises in technique they doubtless taught many lessons to the iron masters and engineers; but they had scarcely anything to contribute to architecture. A later generation built the train sheds for their smoky railways on this pattern; but the precedent lingers today chiefly in subway kiosks and window-fronts, and even here it has created no fresh forms for itself﻿—unless the blank expanse of a plate-glass window framed in metallic grilles can be called a fresh form.

The growth of eclecticism, on the other hand, had by the middle of the century given the American city the aspect of a museum and the American countryside a touch of the picture-book. Washington Irving’s Sunnyside and the first Smithsonian building were in the predominant Gothic mode; but Poe described the mansion of a not altogether imaginary Arnheim as semi-Gothic, semi-Saracenic; and the old Tombs prison in New York got its name from the Egyptian character of its façade. Who can doubt that the design for a *Byzantine* cottage, shown in *The American Cottage Builder* (1854), was somewhere carried out?

Nettled by the criticism that America was not Europe, the pioneer determined to bring Europe to his doors. Relatively few American architects during the period, however, had been abroad, and still fewer had been there to any purpose; even men of culture and imagination like Hawthorne and Emerson were not at home in the physical environment of Europe, however intimate they were with its mind. The buildings that were erected under the inspiration of European tours only accentuated the barbarism of the American scene and the poverty of the architect’s imagination.

===Chinese===
除了铁皮立面和锌皮檐口，欧文那一代人实际看到的，是水晶宫——1853 年仿照伦敦 1850 年那座展览馆在纽约建造的。罗斯金带着讥诮的公道，把原来的水晶宫描述为一座放大了的温室；对这两座建筑，能说的也就到此为止。作为技术练习，它们无疑给炼铁大王和工程师们上了许多课；但它们对建筑几乎无可奉献。后一代人照这个样式为他们烟雾腾腾的铁路建造了列车棚；但这一先例今天主要存留在地铁入口亭和临街橱窗里，即便在这里，它也没有为自己创造出任何新的形式——除非装在金属格栅框里的平板玻璃窗那一大片空白，也能算一种新形式。

另一方面，折衷主义的滋长，到中叶已使美国城市有了博物馆的面貌，美国乡村沾上了图画书的气息。华盛顿·欧文（Washington Irving）的桑尼赛德（Sunnyside）和第一座史密森楼都是占主导地位的哥特式；而爱伦·坡把那座并非全然出于虚构的阿恩海姆府邸描述为半哥特、半萨拉森式；纽约老「墓穴」监狱之名，则得自其立面的埃及风格。《美国村舍营造法》（*The American Cottage Builder*）（1854）里刊有一幅*拜占庭*村舍的设计，谁能怀疑它在某处不曾真个造出来呢？

被「美国不是欧洲」的批评刺痛了，拓荒者决意把欧洲搬到自家门口。然而这一时期的美国建筑师，出过国的相对寥寥，去了而有所收获的更少；就连霍桑（Hawthorne）和爱默生（Emerson）这样有文化、有想象的人，无论对欧洲的精神何等熟稔，在欧洲的物质环境里也并不自在。在欧游启发之下竖立起来的建筑，只是加重了美国景象的蛮荒和建筑师想象力的贫乏。
===Original===
A good part of our architecture today still exhibits the parvenu’s uneasiness, and is by turns French, Italian, or more or less obsolete English; but we do not, perhaps, realize with what a difference; for photography and archaeological research now make it possible to produce buildings which have all the virtues of the original except originality, whereas the earlier, illiterate development of foreign examples, rehearsed in memory, resulted in a conglomerate form which resembled nothing so much, perhaps, as P. T. Barnum’s mermaid.

If the Crystal Palace represents the extreme of industrial art, Colonel Colt’s Armsmear represents the opposite﻿—untutored romanticism. Armsmear was built near Hartford, between 1855 and 1862. A writer in the *Art Journal* for 1876 calls this mansion a “characteristic type of the unique.” It was a “long, grand, impressive, contradictory, beautiful, strange thing.﻿ ﻿… An Italian villa in stone, massive, noble, refined, yet not carrying out any decided principles of architecture, it is like the mind of its originator, bold and unusual in its combinations.﻿ ﻿… There is no doubt it is a little Turkish among other things, on one side it has domes, pinnacles, and light, lavish ornamentation, such as Oriental taste delights in.﻿ ﻿… Yet, although the villa is Italian and cosmopolitan, the feeling is English. It is an English home in its substantiality, its homelike and comfortable aspects.”

===Chinese===
今日我们建筑的一大部分，仍然流露着暴发户的不安，忽而法式，忽而意式，忽而是多少过时的英式；但我们或许没有意识到其中已有天壤之别；因为摄影术和考古学研究如今已能造出兼备原本一切美德、唯独没有独创性的建筑，而早先那种凭记诵排演的外国范本的文盲式发挥，造出的是一种大杂烩的形式——它最像什么，或许莫过于 P. T. 巴纳姆（P. T. Barnum）那具「人鱼」。

如果说水晶宫代表着工业艺术的极点，那么科尔特上校的阿姆斯米尔（Armsmear）代表着另一极——无师自通的浪漫主义。阿姆斯米尔建于哈特福德近郊，1855 至 1862 年间。《艺术杂志》（*Art Journal*）1876 年的一位撰稿人把这座府邸称作「独一类中的典型」。它是「一件长长的、堂皇的、动人的、自相矛盾的、美丽的、古怪的东西。……一座石造的意大利别墅，厚重、高贵、精致，却不遵循任何明确的建筑原则；它像其营造者的头脑一样，组合大胆而不循常轨。……毫无疑问，在别的成分之外，它还带点土耳其味：它一侧有穹顶、尖塔和轻盈挥霍的装饰，正是东方趣味所喜。……然而，这座别墅虽是意大利式且世界主义式的，情调却是英国式的。就其结实、亲切而舒适的面貌而言，它是一个英国人的家。」

===Original===
It is, alas! impossible to illustrate in these pages this remarkable specimen of American architecture; but in a lecture on the “Present and Future Prospects of Chicago” (1846), I have discovered its exact literary equivalent, and it will sum up the crudity and cultural wistfulness of the period perhaps better than any overt description:

> “I thank you [apologizes the lecturer] for the patience you have manifested on this occasion, and promise never more to offend in like manner, so long. I have now, as Cowper observes﻿—
> “And can, I think with Scott, surely say that﻿—
> “I propose now, gentlemen, to leave you at Carlangtoghford,
> “Let me say to you on this occasion, as Campbell does on another:
> “And should you in the contest fall, remember with old Homer﻿—
> “Allow me now to close in one of Scott’s beautiful strains:

===Chinese===
可惜！在本书这几页里，无法为美国建筑的这一出色标本插图；但在一篇题为《芝加哥的现在与未来前景》（1846）的演讲里，我发现了它分毫不差的文字等价物，它或许比任何直白的描述更能概括那个时代的粗陋与文化乡愁：

> 「我感谢诸位〔演讲者道歉道〕在今日场合所表现的耐心，并保证绝不再以类似方式、这么久地冒犯诸位。我如今，正如库柏（Cowper）所言——
> 「而我能够，我想随司各特（Scott）一道，笃定地说——
> 「诸位，我现在提议，在卡兰托格福德把诸位留下，
> 「让我在这个场合对诸位说一句，正如坎贝尔（Campbell）在另一场合所说：
> 「而倘若你们在竞赛中倒下，请和年迈的荷马（Homer）一起记住——
> 「现在请允许我以司各特一段美妙的诗章作结：
===Original===
*That* was American architecture between 1820 and the Civil War﻿—a collection of tags, thrown at random against a building. Architectural forms were brought together by a mere juxtaposition of materials, held in place by neither imagination nor logic. There are a number of honorable exceptions to this rule, for architects like Renwick, who designed St. Patrick’s Cathedral, and Upjohn, who built Trinity Church, had a more sincere understanding of the conventional task; and by any standard of esthetic decency the old Gothic building of New York University, on Washington Square, was a far finer structure than the bulky office building that has taken its place. Nevertheless, this saving remnant does not alter the character of the great mass of work, any more than the occasionally excellent cast-iron balconies, brought over from the London of the Regency, alter the depressing character of the great mass of domestic building. In elevation and interior treatment, these antebellum buildings were all what-nots. Souvenirs of architecture, their forms dimly recall the monuments of the past without in any sense taking their place.

To tell the truth, a pall had fallen over the industrial city: contemporary writers in the ’forties and ’fifties speak of the filth and smoke, and without doubt the chocolate brownstone front was introduced as a measure of protective coloration. In this dingy environment, men turned to nature as a refuge against the soiled and bedraggled works of man’s creation; and as the creeping factory and railroad train removed Nature farther from their doors, the park was introduced as a more convenient means of escape. The congested capitals of Europe had already learnt this lesson; traveled Americans, like William Cullen Bryant, brought it home; and Central Park, planned in 1853, was the first of the great landscape parks to serve as a people’s pleasance. Conceived in contrast to the deflowered landscape and the muddled city, the park alone recreated the traditions of civilization﻿—of man naturalized, and therefore at home, of nature humanized, and therefore enriched. And even today our parks are what our cities should be, and are not.

By 1860 the halcyon day of American civilization was over; the spirit had lingered in letters and scholarship, in the work of Parkman and Motley and Emerson and Melville and Thoreau, but the sun had already sunk below the horizon, and what seemed a promise was in reality an afterglow. By the time the Civil War came, architecture had recorded faithfully the social transformation; it was sullen, grim, gauche, unstable. While in almost every age architecture has an independent value to the spirit, so that we can rejoice in Chartres or Winchester even though we have abandoned the Roman faith, in the early industrial period architecture is reduced to a symptom. Romanticism had not restored the past, nor had industrialism made the future more welcome. Architecture wandered between two worlds, “one dead, the other powerless to be born.”

===Chinese===
*那*便是 1820 年到内战之间的美国建筑——一堆随手甩到建筑上去的引语碎屑。建筑形式仅凭材料的并置拼在一处，既无想象力也无逻辑加以维系。这条规则有若干可敬的例外：像设计圣帕特里克大教堂的伦威克（Renwick）、建造三一教堂的厄普约翰（Upjohn），对这一因袭的任务便有较为诚恳的领会；而且无论以何种审美体面的标准衡量，华盛顿广场上纽约大学的那座老哥特楼，也远比取它而代之的臃肿办公楼精美。尽管如此，这一点得救的余数，改变不了大批作品的品性，正如偶尔从摄政时期伦敦运来的精美铸铁阳台，改变不了大批住宅建筑那令人抑郁的品性。论立面与室内处理，这些内战前的建筑都是些百宝架。它们是建筑的纪念品，其形式隐约令人追想往昔的纪念碑，却绝无取而代之的分量。

说实在的，一层殓布早已罩住了工业城市：四五十年代的作家们讲到污秽与煤烟，而巧克力色的褐砂石立面之被引入，无疑是一种保护色措施。在这昏暗的环境里，人们转向自然，以躲避人手所造的那些玷污狼藉的作品；而随着悄悄爬行的工厂和火车把自然越推越远，公园便被引入，作为一种更便利的出逃手段。拥挤的欧洲都城早已领教过这一课；游历过的美国人，如威廉·卡伦·布莱恩特（William Cullen Bryant），把它带回了家；1853 年规划的中央公园，是第一座用作人民游憩地的大型风景公园。它是在被蹂躏的景观和紊乱的城市的对照中构想出来的，唯有公园再造了文明的传统——人归化于自然，因而安然处之；自然被人化，因而丰饶充实。直到今天，我们的公园仍是我们城市本该成为而未曾成为的样子。

到 1860 年，美国文明的太平盛世已然告终；精神还在书信与学术里、在帕克曼（Parkman）、莫特利（Motley）、爱默生、梅尔维尔（Melville）和梭罗（Thoreau）的著作中流连，但太阳早已沉入地平线下，看似允诺的，实则已是落日的余晖。到内战来临时，建筑已忠实记录了这场社会转型：它阴沉、严酷、笨拙、飘摇。几乎在任何一个时代，建筑对精神都有独立的价值，以致我们即便抛弃了罗马公教，也仍能欣享沙特尔或温彻斯特；而在工业早期，建筑却被贬成了一个症候。浪漫主义没有恢复过去，工业主义也没有使未来更受欢迎。建筑游荡在两个世界之间，「一个已死，另一个无力出生」。

## II The Heritage of the Renaissance / 文艺复兴的遗产

### I / 一

===Original===
The forces that undermined the medieval civilization of Europe sapped the vitality from the little centers it had deposited in America. What happened in the course of three or four centuries in Europe took scarcely a hundred years on this side of the Atlantic.

Economically and culturally, the village community had been pretty well self-contained; it scraped along on its immediate resources, and if it could not purchase for itself the "best of everything" it at least made the most of what it had. In every detail of house construction, from the setting of fireplaces to the slope of the roof, there were local peculiarities which distinguished not merely the Dutch settlements from the English, but which even characterized several settlements in Rhode Island that were scarcely a day's tramp apart. The limitation of materials, and the carpenter's profound ignorance of "style" made for freedom and diversity. It remained for the eighteenth century to erect a single canon of taste.

With the end of the seventeenth century the economic basis of provincial life shifted from the farm to the sea. This change had the same effect upon New England, where the village-community proper alone had flourished, that fur-trading had had upon New York: it broke up the internal unity of the village by giving separate individuals the opportunity by what was literally a "lucky haul," to achieve a position of financial superiority. Fishermen are the miners of the water. Instead of the long, watchful care that the farmer must exercise from planting time to harvest, fishing demands a sharp eye and a quick, hard stroke of work; and since what the Germans call *Sitzfleisch* is not one of the primary qualities of a free lad, it is no wonder that the sea weaned the young folks of New England away from the drudgeries of its boulder-strewn farms. With fishing, trading, and building wooden vessels for sale in foreign ports, riches poured into maritime New England; and what followed scarcely needs an explanation.

These villages ceased to be communities of farmers, working the land and standing squarely on their own soil: they became commercial towns which, instead of trading for a living, simply lived for trade. With this change, castes arose; first, the division between the poor and the rich, and then between craftsmen and merchants, between the independent workers and the menials. The common concerns of all the townsfolk took second rank: the privileges of the great landlords and merchants warped the development of the community. Boston, by the middle of the eighteenth century, was rich in public buildings, including four schoolhouses, seventeen churches, a Town House, a Province House, and Faneuil Hall—a pretty large collection for a town whose twenty thousand inhabitants would scarcely fill a single block of tenements in the Bronx. But by this time a thousand inhabitants were set down as poor, and an almshouse and a workhouse had been provided for them.
===Chinese===
削弱了欧洲中世纪文明的那些力量，同样把生命力从它播植在美洲的那些小小中心里抽走了。在欧洲历时三四百年方才完成的过程，在大西洋的这一边，几乎不到一百年便告完成。

无论在经济上还是文化上，村庄共同体都相当自给自足；它靠手边的资源勉力维持，纵然买不起「样样最好的东西」，至少也把手头的东西用到了十足。住宅营造的每一个细节，从壁炉的位置到屋顶的坡度，都带着地方特色，这不仅使荷兰人的定居点有别于英国人的，甚至也让罗得岛（Rhode Island）内几个相去不过一日步程的定居点各具面目。材料的匮乏，加上木匠对「风格」的全然无知，反倒造就了自由与多样。要立起一条统一的趣味法则，还有待十八世纪。

随着十七世纪的终结，外省生活的经济基础从农田移向了海洋。这一变化之于新英格兰——严格意义上的村庄共同体唯一兴盛过的地方——恰如皮毛贸易之于纽约：它让个别人有机会凭一次名副其实的「幸运一网」取得财力上的优越地位，从而拆散了村庄内部的统一。渔民是水上的矿工。农夫从播种到收获，须付出漫长而须臾不离的照料；捕鱼却只要求一双锐利的眼睛和一下迅疾而用力的劳作；既然自由的小伙子并不把德国人所谓的 *Sitzfleisch*（安坐苦干的耐性）当作头等品质，海洋把新英格兰的年轻人从遍布乱石的农场的苦役中引诱开去，也就不足为怪了。捕鱼、经商，再建造木船售往外国口岸，财富随之滚滚涌入濒海的新英格兰；此后的种种，几乎无需解释。

这些村庄不再是耕种土地、稳稳站立在自己土壤上的农民共同体：它们变成了商业城镇，不再靠贸易谋生，而是干脆为贸易而活。随着这一转变，等级应运而生：先是穷人与富人之分，继而是工匠与商人之分、独立劳动者与仆役之分。全体镇民共同的关切退居次位：大地产主与大商人的特权扭曲了共同体的发展。到十八世纪中叶，波士顿（Boston）的公共建筑已堪称丰富，包括四所校舍、十七座教堂、一座市政厅（Town House）、一座省府大厦（Province House），还有法尼尔厅（Faneuil Hall）——对一座两万居民尚且填不满布朗克斯（the Bronx）一个街区长屋的城镇来说，这已是一笔相当可观的收藏。但此时已有一千居民被登记为贫民，并且已经为他们设起了济贫院和习艺所。
===Original===
With the rise of the merchant class, the industrial guild began to weaken, as it had weakened in Europe during the Renaissance. For about a hundred years the carpenter-builder continued to remain on the scene, and work in his forthright and painstaking and honest manner; but in the middle of the eighteenth century he was joined, for the first time, by the professional architect, the first one being probably Peter Harrison, who designed the Redwood Library, which still stands in Newport. Under competition with architects and amateurs of taste, the carpenter-builder lost his position as an independent craftsman, building intelligently for his equals: he was forced to meet the swift, corrosive influences brought in from foreign lands by men who had visited the ports of the world; and he must set his sails in order to catch the new winds of fashion.

What were these winds, and what effect did they have upon the architecture of the time?

Most of the influences that came by way of trade affected only the accent of architecture; the language remained a homely vernacular. In the middle of the eighteenth century China sent over wallpaper; and in the Metropolitan Museum there is an American lacquered cabinet dated as early as 1700, decorated with obscure little Chinese figures in gilded gesso. "China" itself came in to take the place of pewter and earthenware in the finer houses; while in the gardens of the great manors, pavilions and pagodas, done more or less in the Chinese manner, were fashionable. Even Thomas Jefferson, with his impeccably classical taste, designed such a pavilion for Monticello before the Revolution.

This specific Chinese influence was part of that large, eclectic Oriental influence of the eighteenth century. The cultural spirit that produced Montesquieu's *Lettres Persanes* also led to the translation of the Chinese and Persian and Sanskrit classics, and by a more direct route brought home Turkish dressing-gowns, turbans, and slippers to Boston merchants. In Copley's painting of Nicholas Boylston, in 1767, these Turkish ornaments rise comically against the suggestion of a Corinthian pillar in the background; and this pillar recalls to us the principal influence of the time—that of classic civilization. This influence entered America first as a motif in decoration, and passed out only after it had become a dominating motive in life.
===Chinese===
随着商人阶层的兴起，行业行会开始削弱，一如文艺复兴期间它在欧洲的削弱。此后约莫一百年，木匠兼营造师仍在现场延续，以他那种直率、勤勉而诚实的方式做工；但到十八世纪中叶，头一回有职业建筑师加入进来，第一人大概当推彼得·哈里森（Peter Harrison）——新港（Newport）的红木图书馆（Redwood Library）即出其手笔，至今犹存。在与建筑师和趣味行家的竞争中，木匠兼营造师失去了他作为独立工匠、为本阶层中人明智营造的位置：他不得不去应付那些周游世界各口岸的人从异邦带回的迅疾而具侵蚀性的影响；他还得张起船帆，去兜住时尚的新风。

这些风是什么？它们又给当时的建筑带来了什么影响？

循贸易之道而来的种种影响，大多只触到建筑的口音；语言依旧是一种家常的土语。十八世纪中叶，中国送来了墙纸；大都会博物馆（Metropolitan Museum）藏有一件美国漆柜，年代早至 1700 年，上面饰着镀金石膏底子上影影绰绰的小小中国人像。「瓷器」（China）本身也进了上等人家，取代了白镴器与陶器；而在大庄园的园林里，多少依中国式样造的亭榭与宝塔蔚为时尚。就连古典趣味无可指摘的托马斯·杰斐逊（Thomas Jefferson），也在独立革命之前为蒙蒂塞洛（Monticello）设计过这样一座亭子。

这一具体的中国影响，属于十八世纪那个宏大而兼收并蓄的东方影响的一部分。孕育了孟德斯鸠（Montesquieu）《波斯人信札》（Lettres Persanes）的那种文化精神，也推动了中国、波斯与梵文经典的翻译，并且循一条更直接的路径，把土耳其晨衣、缠头巾和拖鞋带回了波士顿商人的家中。在科普利（Copley）1767 年所绘的尼古拉斯·博伊尔斯顿（Nicholas Boylston）肖像里，这些土耳其行头衬着背景中隐约的一根科林斯柱，显得颇为滑稽；而这根柱子又让我们想起那个时代最主要的影响——古典文明的影响。这一影响最初是作为装饰母题进入美国的，直到它变成生活中占支配地位的动机之后，方才退场。

### II / 二
===Original===
The Renaissance was an orientation of the European mind towards the forms of Roman and Greek civilization, and towards the meaning of classical culture. On the latter side its impulse was plainly a liberating one: it delivered the human soul from a cell of torments in which there were no modulating interests or activities between the base satisfactions of the temporal life and the beatitudes of heaven. With the Renaissance the god-beast became, once again, a man. Moreover, just when the Catholic culture of Christendom was breaking down under the influence of heresy and skepticism, the classics brought to the educated men of Europe a common theme which saved them from complete intellectual vagrancy.
